[01:40:22.688] feasibility of redeveloping these vacant and tax-delinquent properties by
[01:40:26.368] expanding development possibilities beyond single-family homes.
[01:40:30.148] So the question is, why change the RS zoning?
[01:40:32.628] This is essentially an economics question.
[01:40:35.348] The cost to construct a new single-family home in the city of Chicago starts at
[01:40:38.148] about $500,000. Without subsidy, the purchase price of that
[01:40:42.048] tends to be around $700,000 or more.
[01:40:44.948] So this is only affordable to households making approximately
[01:40:48.648] $180,000 a year, which is almost twice the area median income
[01:40:53.228] for the Chicago area.
[01:40:58.008] A new construction single-family home also costs twice as much as existing homes in
[01:41:01.768] neighborhoods like Auburn Gresham and Chatham, so there's not much of a market,
[01:41:05.628] really, for new construction of single-family homes in this area.
[01:41:09.428] Virtually all of the market rate construction that DPD has facilitated through
[01:41:13.288] land sales has been in RT-4 and RM-5 districts on the south side.
[01:41:17.308] It's also difficult to finance the renovation of non-conforming buildings like
[01:41:20.948] two-flats or three-flats when the zoning is RS.
[01:41:24.528] It's just a lending issue. It's easier to get lending to deconvert that to a
[01:41:27.768] single-family home than to maintain the two units.
[01:41:29.908] So that directly leads to a loss of housing units and gentrification.
[01:41:35.408] This is the recommended zoning, again, to RT-4.

[02:28:57.482] The applicant is before the committee today seeking its favorable recommendation
[02:29:01.542] on a planned development application for the redevelopment of the
[02:29:05.202] approximately 67-acre Ford City Mall site, located at
[02:29:09.982] 7601 South Cicero Avenue.
[02:29:13.062] The application was considered by the Chicago Plan Commission at its August 20th
[02:29:16.802] hearing and received a favorable recommendation.
[02:29:19.562] We would respectfully request that the transcript and record of the Plan Commission
[02:29:23.162] proceedings be incorporated into the record here today.
[02:29:26.922] This item was heard and passed by the Chicago Plan Commission.
[02:29:30.602] Alderman Spata makes a motion to incorporate all records and findings from that
[02:29:33.822] hearing. All in favor, please say aye. Aye. All opposed say nay.
[02:29:37.062] In the opinion of the chair, the ayes have it. The records are incorporated.
[02:29:40.082] Thank you.
[02:29:41.382] The property is generally bounded by a private road known as
[02:29:45.082] West 74th Street and the Tootsie Roll property on the
[02:29:48.842] north, Kostner Avenue on the east, a private road known as
[02:29:52.842] 76th Street on the south, and Cicero Avenue on the west.
[02:29:57.342] The requested zoning change is from the existing
[02:29:59.942] B3-2 community shopping district to an
[02:30:03.922] M2-2 light industry district, and then to an industrial planned
[02:30:07.902] development. Because the site exceeds the
[02:30:11.262] applicable acreage threshold for industrial development on M-zoned
[02:30:15.082] land,
[02:30:16.562] this was a mandatory planned development.
[02:30:21.722] Nicole, is somebody going to progress through the slides? Thank you.
[02:30:25.762] The property includes the now vacant Ford City Mall and the surrounding
[02:30:30.582] surface parking lots. The site's history is closely tied to
[02:30:33.902] industrial development. During World War II, it was part of the
[02:30:37.762] massive Dodge Chicago plant, which constructed aircraft
[02:30:41.542] engines for the war effort. Ford Motor Company later
[02:30:45.402] operated at the property, which gave Ford City its name.
[02:30:49.122] A portion of the former industrial complex was subsequently
[02:30:52.622] redeveloped as the Ford City Mall,
[02:30:55.362] which opened in 1965.
[02:30:58.522] In recent decades, the mall experienced significant decline and
[02:31:02.122] disinvestment. Today, it is completely vacant and
[02:31:06.122] the property has deteriorated to the point that the mall was ordered to be
[02:31:09.562] closed due to public health and safety concerns.
[02:31:14.062] The proposal before you today would replace the vacant and
[02:31:17.722] obsolete property with a modern industrial campus and
[02:31:21.642] would return this historically industrial site to a productive economic use.
[02:31:27.442] Curve Industrial is the contract purchaser of the property and proposes to
[02:31:31.442] demolish the existing mall, undertake environmental
[02:31:35.062] remediation necessary to redevelop the site, and construct four
[02:31:39.022] new Class A industrial buildings totaling approximately
[02:31:42.622] 1.1 million square feet in floor area.
[02:31:46.582] The buildings are being developed on a speculative basis, so no specific end
[02:31:50.662] users have been identified yet. The buildings are designed to accommodate
[02:31:54.382] modern industrial logistics, distribution, and manufacturing
[02:31:58.222] users consistent with the proposed zoning and the planned development.
[02:32:02.642] The development has been comprehensively master planned as a campus with
[02:32:06.582] truck loading, employee parking, and internal circulation organized
[02:32:10.422] within the site to minimize impacts on surrounding properties.
[02:32:14.542] Particular attention has been given to separating truck traffic from passenger
[02:32:18.382] vehicles wherever possible and providing safe and efficient circulation
[02:32:22.282] throughout the development
[02:32:24.470] Importantly, this is more than the construction of four new buildings.
[02:32:28.130] The redevelopment includes substantial investment in infrastructure that serves
[02:32:31.750] both the property and the surrounding area, including a new CTA bus
[02:32:35.770] terminal, new and reconstructed roadways and sidewalks,
[02:32:39.970] new utilities, and significant water and sewer
[02:32:43.970] infrastructure.
[02:32:45.910] The project also includes approximately 913 new
[02:32:49.530] trees and more than eight acres of native landscaping and stormwater
[02:32:53.550] management improvements.
[02:32:55.510] The property currently lacks modern stormwater management
[02:32:58.970] infrastructure.
[02:33:01.050] The development will also introduce
[02:33:03.470] a comprehensive stormwater management system that's designed to substantially
[02:33:06.850] improve conditions on the site and reduce potential for flooding.
[02:33:11.050] The project will be designed to achieve LEED Gold certification and will comply
[02:33:14.750] with the city's sustainable development policy,
[02:33:17.650] achieving 100 points from that sustainable development policy matrix.
[02:33:22.190] The development will also establish the Ford City Heritage Plaza,
[02:33:26.070] a new publicly accessible landscaped gathering space that will
[02:33:29.550] recognize the unique history and evolution of the Ford City property.
[02:33:34.290] Altogether, the development represents an investment of
[02:33:37.830] approximately $200 million and is anticipated to create
[02:33:41.490] approximately 185 permanent jobs and 90
[02:33:45.010] construction jobs. The developer has also established MBE and
[02:33:48.950] WBE participation goals of 26% and
[02:33:52.830] 6% respectively.
[02:33:55.650] The application was filed in July 2025.
[02:33:59.130] The project has undergone more than a year of extensive community,
[02:34:03.230] stakeholder, and departmental reviews.
[02:34:05.890] During that time, the development team worked closely with the Department
[02:34:09.850] of Planning and Development, CDOT, the Department of Water Management, the
[02:34:13.830] Department of Buildings, the law department, as well as the CTA
[02:34:18.230] to address infrastructure, transportation, and operational issues
[02:34:22.250] associated with the redevelopment of this unusually large
[02:34:26.250] and complex site.
[02:34:28.130] Alderman Curtis hosted community meetings in September 2025 and

[04:00:50.760] on Belden Avenue to educational use, which is a permitted use
[04:00:54.680] under the building's current zoning.

[04:01:04.840] lightly. In 2025, Parker presented a previous version of this
[04:01:08.620] plan to nearby homeowners and the Mid-North Association.

[04:01:20.100] revisions and additional community benefits.

[04:01:27.180] preservation of residential housing on Belden.

[04:01:34.639] this past March.
